Alternatively, compute S_i by `np.convolve(p, w, mode='full')`? We derived p[::-1]. Let's implement a helper:

def cross_sum_lags(p, w):
    # p len n, w len 2n-1
    # S_i = sum_{j=0}^{n-1} p[j] * w[i+j]
    # Use FFT: conv(p[::-1], w)[n-1 : 2n-1]
    Np=len(p); Nw=len(w)
    M = 1 << (Np+Nw-2).bit_length()
    Fp = np.fft.rfft(p[::-1], M)
    Fw = np.fft.rfft(w, M)
    conv = np.fft.irfft(Fp*Fw, M)
    return conv[Np-1:Np-1+Np]

Check length: Nw=2N-1, conv length 3N-2? Wait p length N, w length 2N-1 => conv len N+2N-1-1=3N-2. Indices N-1 .. 2N-2 inclusive length N. Good. In formula L_w? w indices 0..2N-2. i+N-1 max 2N-2. OK.

- p softmax(y)

- mean_c = dot(w,c)

Implementation details:

Fast convolution score:
```
def normalize_nonneg(a):
    a = np.asarray(a, dtype=np.float64)
    a = np.where(a>0, a, 0.0)
    s = a.sum()
    if s <= 0: return np.ones(len(a))/len(a), True
    return a/s, False
```
But for search we want allow tiny. For final, ensure list floats nonnegative.

Convolution via FFT:
```
def conv_full(x):
    n=len(x)
    if n < 64:
        return np.convolve(x,x)
    M=1
    need=2*n-1
    while M < need: M <<=1
    X=np.fft.rfft(x, M)
    conv=np.fft.irfft(X*X, M)[:need]
    # due small negative? clip min 0? For max not issue; but weights c maybe negative tiny. Use np.maximum(conv,0) after?
    return np.maximum(conv, 0.0)
```
Clipping small negatives to 0 may slightly change sum/average but fine. For exact score, evaluator uses np.convolve without clipping; but if negative due FFT -1e-18, max unaffected. If we clip, score maybe same. Use `np.maximum(conv,0)` to ensure weights nonnegative. Sum may be off; normalization of p already sum1, conv sum should1; clipping tiny no matter.

Score:
```
def score(p):
    c=conv_full(p)
    return 2*len(p)*float(c.max()) if p.sum()>0 else inf
```
Since p normalized.

For many evaluations, computing FFT length pow2 each time overhead. We can cache M? Since N fixed within local search, implement a class? But helper functions top-level no closures; can use global dict keyed by n? Simpler: use `next_fast_len(n)` with bit_length. For same N repeated, okay. `M = 1 << (2*n-1 -1).bit_length()`.

Top-lag update function:
```
def momentum_update(p, c, rng, lr_scale, max_top=64):
    n=len(p); L=len(c)
    # near max indices
    cmax=c.max()
    # choose threshold: c >= cmax - max(1e-12, cmax*1e-5)
    near = c >= cmax - max(1e-12, cmax*1e-5)
    if near.sum() ==0: near = np.array([int(np.argmax(c))])
    top_count = min(near.sum(), max(8, n//8, 32)) # maybe max_top
    idx = np.flatnonzero(near)
    if len(idx)>top_count:
       # sample with replacement? no replacement
       if rng.random()<0.5: idx=idx[:top_count]
       else: idx=rng.choice(idx, size=top_count, replace=False)
    # weights could be proportional to c? Use 1 or c? Use 1 to focus; maybe weighted by c-cmin.
    S=np.zeros(n)
    for k in idx:
       k=int(k)
       lo=max(0, k-n+1); hi=min(n-1,k)
       if hi>=lo:
          S[lo:hi+1] += p[k-hi:k-lo+1][::-1]
    mean = c[idx].mean() # or cmax
    G = n*(S/len(idx) - mean)  # if uniform weights average S and mean
    return G
```
But if S/len(idx) vs mean: For weights uniform over chosen lags, S_i=mean of p_{k-i}. `mean` should be average c over those lags, not cmax. Objective gradient for average c_U: f_i=2S_i; Euler ∑p f_i=2 mean_c. So G = S/|U| - mean. Yes.

Floor renormalization:
```
def apply_step(p, G, lr):
    n=p.size
    step = np.exp(-lr*G)
    q = p*step
    # floor epsilon relative maybe 1e-12 to keep all positive
    s=sum(q)
    if s<=0: return p
    q = q/s
    # optional floor: eps=1e-12; q=(1-eps)*q + eps/n? This can slow. Use only if min==0.
    if q.min() <= 0:
       q = q + 1e-15; q/=q.sum()
    return q
```
Since p positive, step positive, no zero unless underflow. G finite. Use `np.clip(G, -50, 50)` to avoid exp overflow.

   start=...

   # maybe shift mass: normalize; sometimes sym or reverse? Reversing preserves score? Actually score of reverse same? A_rev^2 coefficients are reversed? Max same. So no need.
   # Add small global noise to log: q*=exp(normal(0,0.05)); renorm.
   # Occasional basin: block set to geometric/power pattern.
```

Local search loop for a base p:
```
def local_search(p0, time_limit, rng, name):
   p=normalize(p0)
   best=p.copy(); best_score=score(p)
   no_improve=0
   while time < t0+time_limit:
      c=conv_full(p); sc=2*n*c.max()
      if sc<best_score: best... no_improve=0 else no_improve+=1
      G = compute_G(p,c,rng)
      improved=False
      for lr in lrs:
         q=apply_step(p,G,lr)
         s=score(q)
         if s<best_score: best=q; best_score=s; p=q; improved=True; break
      if not improved:
         # try random perturbations few
         if no_improve>...:
            strengths = [0.05,0.1,0.2,0.5]
            for strength: q=perturb(p,rng,strength); if score(q)<best... accept; else maybe accept with probability? keep best.
            if no improvement after many, break to new restart
   return best,best_score
```
But if local search stuck, random restart from best with more noise. We can call local_search with different initial p.

def make_power_series(n, alpha, shift, increasing=False):
   i=np.arange(n,dtype=float)
   if increasing: x=i+shift; vals=x**(alpha) maybe negative? For power singular: x**(-alpha)
   vals=np.exp(-alpha*np.log(x+1e-300))

- `softmax(v, floor_frac=0)`:
```
v = np.asarray(v,float)
v = v - v.max()
z = np.exp(v)
s=z.sum()
if s<=0 or not finite: return np.ones(n)/n
p=z/s
if floor_frac>0: p=(1-floor_frac)*p + floor_frac/n
return p
```
- `logp(p)`: `np.log(np.maximum(p, 1e-300))`.

- `make_base_power(n, alpha, shift, increasing, rng, mix_rev=0)`
```
i=np.arange(n,float)
if increasing:
   x=i+shift
   v=x**(alpha)
else:
   x=i+shift
   v=x**(-alpha)
# sometimes add small linear ramp/skew
v = np.maximum(v, 1e-6)
if mix_rev>0: v = (1-mix_rev)*v + mix_rev*v[::-1]
# optionally random smooth skew y
return normalize(v)
```
But for stable, use logs: `v=np.exp(-alpha*np.log(i+shift))` for dec; `np.exp(alpha*np.log(i+shift))` for inc. Alpha can be negative? We'll call separately.

- `mirror_local_search(p0, time_limit, rng, print_name='')` returns best p, score.
   Details:
```
p = sanitize(p0); n=len(p)
best_p=p.copy(); best_score=score_norm(p)
start=time.monotonic()
it=0
noimp=0
while time.monotonic() < start+time_limit and noimp < max_noimp:
    it+=1
    c=conv_fast(p)
    sc=2.0*n*float(c.max())  # p sum 1
    if sc+1e-12 < best_score:
       best_score=sc; best_p=p.copy(); noimp=0
    else:
       noimp += 1
    # Choose step
    G = compute_subgradient(p,c,rng)
    if G is None: break
    improved=False
    # Step sizes maybe scaled with n? G already scaled mean O(1). Use lrs adaptive
    lrs = [0.1,0.3,1.0,3.0,10.0]
    # random order? try smaller first? For convergence small.
    for lr in lrs:
       q = exp_step(p,G,lr)
       sq = score_norm(q)
       if sq < best_score - 1e-12:
          best_score=sq; best_p=q.copy(); p=q; improved=True; break
    if not improved:
       # try a few random perturbations around p
       for strength in [0.02,0.05,0.1,0.2]:
          q = random_perturb(p,rng,strength)
          sq=score_norm(q)
          if sq < best_score - 1e-12:
             best_score=sq; best_p=q.copy(); p=q; improved=True
       if not improved:
          # occasionally accept a slightly worse candidate (simulated annealing) to escape
          if noimp > 5 and rng.random() < 0.1:
             q = exp_step(p,G, rng.uniform(0.2,2.0))
             # if not too worse, accept to explore; keep best
             p=q
          noimp += 1
          # if noimp large, restart from best with noise
          if noimp > max_noimp: break
# after loop, return best_p,best_score
```
Need `score_norm(p)` computes conv and exact max. It normalizes? p assumed normalized; but after exp_step renormalized. Use `score_general` to be safe.

`compute_subgradient(p,c,rng)`:
```
n=len(p); L=c.size; cmax=float(c.max())
# near max
tol = max(1e-15, cmax*1e-6)
near = c >= cmax - tol
idx = np.flatnonzero(near)
if idx.size ==0: idx=np.array([int(np.argmax(c))])
# target count
target = n if n<32 else max(16, min(128, n//8))
if idx.size > target:
    # if many, random subset, biased to highest c
    # sort idx by c descending? startup okay
    # Use rng.choice
    idx = rng.choice(idx, size=target, replace=False)
# Add occasional random extras to avoid cycles
if rng.random()<0.2 and idx.size<target*2:
    extra = rng.integers(0,L,min(target//2, L))
    idx = np.unique(np.concatenate((idx,extra)))
# Compute S average
S=np.zeros(n)
cnt=len(idx)
for k in idx:
   k=int(k)
   lo=max(0,k-n+1); hi=min(n-1,k)
   if hi>=lo:
      S[lo:hi+1] += p[k-hi:k-lo+1][::-1]
S /= float(cnt)
mean_c = float(c[idx].mean())
G = n*(S - mean_c)
# If all G zero, add tiny random
if not np.any(G): rng.normal(0,0.01,n)
return np.clip(G, -100,100)
```
But if c values differ, weighting by c could be better. If we choose top, mean_c close cmax. S average. The gradient scaling for average c_U: f_i=2S_i, Euler=2 mean_U. Yes.
If use random extras lower c, mean includes them, effectively smoothing.

`exp_step(p,G,lr)`:
```
q = p * np.exp(-lr*G)
s=q.sum()
if not finite or s<=0: return p
q/=s
# floor to allow recovery
if q.min() == 0:
   q = q + 1e-15
   q /= q.sum()
return q
```
Potential issue: if G has positive mean unweighted, exp step changes total mass distribution but renormalization okay. Need clip G to -50,50 to avoid exp overflow. If G=100, lr=10 overflow. Use clip 20.

`random_perturb(p,rng,strength)`:
```
q=p.copy()
# log perturbation
logp=np.log(np.maximum(q,1e-300))
# global noise
logp += rng.normal(0,strength*0.3, q.size)
# block multiplicative
n=q.size
nb=int(rng.integers(1, max(2, n//8/+1)))
for _ in range(nb):
   width=int(rng.integers(max(2,n//20), max(3,n//4)))
   start=int(rng.integers(0,max(1,n-width)))
   logp[start:start+width] += rng.normal(0, strength)
q=np.exp(logp - logp.max()); s=q.sum(); if s>0 q/=s
# slight floor
return q + min_frac? renorm
```

LP helper:
```
def lp_direction(f, method='highs'):
    n=f.size
    M = float(np.convolve(f,f).max())
    # construct A rows maybe using sparse? scipy sparse not required? linprog accepts dense.
    A = np.zeros((2*n-1,n))
    # A[k,i]=f[k-i] valid. We can fill by loops over i? n=128 ok.
    for i,f_i in enumerate(f):
       if f_i==0: continue
       # k from i to i+n-1
       A[i:i+n, i] = f
    # But A_{k,i} = f_{k-i}; as k increases, j=k-i from0 to n-1 => f[0..n-1]. So column i is f shifted down by i. Yes.
    c_obj = -np.ones(n)
    res = linprog(c_obj, A_ub=A, b_ub=np.full(2*n-1, M), bounds=[(0,None)]*n, method='highs')
    if res.status==0: g=res.x; g=np.maximum(g,0); s=g.sum(); if s>0 g/=s; else None
    return g
```

Now, Nelder-Mead search helper:
```
def nelder_mead_search(p0, max_time, rng=None, maxfev=3000):
    y = np.log(np.maximum(p0,1e-15))
    start=time.monotonic()
    # We can implement simple Nelder-Mead ourselves? scipy easier.
    try:
       from scipy.optimize import minimize
    except: return p0,score
    def fun(vec):
       p=softmax(vec, floor_frac=1e-10)
       return score_norm(p)
    x0=y - y.mean()
    res = minimize(fun, x0, method='Nelder-Mead', options={'maxiter': int(maxfev*10), 'maxfev': maxfev, 'xatol':1e-8, 'fatol':1e-8, 'adaptive':True})
    p=softmax(res.x, floor_frac=1e-10)
    return p, score_norm(p)
```

Helper `clean_sequence(a)`:
```
def clean_sequence(a):
    arr = np.asarray(a, dtype=np.float64).ravel()
    if arr.size == 0:
        return np.ones(64, dtype=np.float64)/64
    arr[~np.isfinite(arr)] = 0.0
    arr = np.maximum(arr, 0.0)
    s = arr.sum()
    if s <= 1e-15:
        return np.ones(arr.size)/arr.size
    arr = arr / s
    return arr
```
But for final we want sum1; for evaluator scaling irrelevant. For search, normalized. If input length 0, length 64.

`score_normalized(p)`:
```
def score_normalized(p):
    p = np.asarray(p, dtype=np.float64)
    n = p.size
    s = p.sum()
    if n == 0 or s <= 1e-15 or not np.all(np.isfinite(p)):
        return np.inf
    q = p / s
    c = convolution_fast(q)
    return float(2.0 * n * np.max(c))
```
For speed, in local search p already normalized; avoid re-sum each time? This function necessary for candidates. Could make `score_norm_fast(p)` assuming normalized; but clean inputs. Use one.

`convolution_fast(a)`:
```
def convolution_fast(a):
    a = np.asarray(a, dtype=np.float64).ravel()
    n=a.size
    need=2*n-1
    if n <= 512:
       c=np.convolve(a,a)
    else:
       M=1 << (need-1).bit_length()
       fa=np.fft.rfft(a, M)
       c=np.fft.irfft(fa*fa, M)[:need]
    c = np.maximum(c, 0.0)
    return c
```
For n=512 direct 262k; okay. Maybe threshold 256. Use 409? We can set 384.

`softmax_vec(v, floor=0.0)`:
```
def softmax_vec(v, floor=0.0):
    v=np.asarray(v,float)
    n=v.size
    if n==0: return np.array([])
    v=v-v.max()
    z=np.exp(v)
    s=z.sum()
    if not np.isfinite(s) or s<=0:
        p=np.ones(n)/n
    else:
        p=z/s
    if floor>0:
        p = (1.0-floor)*p + floor/n
    return p
```
Floor default 0.

`log_sequence(p, minv=1e-300)`: `np.log(np.clip(p, minv, None))`.

`base_power(n, alpha, shift, increasing=False)`:
```
def base_power(n, alpha, shift, increasing=False):
    i=np.arange(n,dtype=float)
    x=i+max(shift,1e-6)
    if increasing:
       v=np.exp(alpha*np.log(x))  # x^alpha
    else:
       v=np.exp(-alpha*np.log(x))
    v=np.maximum(v,1e-300)
    s=v.sum()
    if s<=0: v=np.ones(n)/n
    return v/s
```
Alpha can be negative? Avoid.
`base_random` uses log trend:
```
def base_random(n, rng, strength=1.0):
    a=rng.uniform(-1,1); b=rng.uniform(-1,1)
    y=a*n*np.linspace(0,1,n) + b*strength*rng.normal(0,1,n)
    return softmax_vec(y, 0.0)
```

i=np.arange(n); v=r**i
if r close1, use exp(log(r)*i)
normalize

`compute_subgradient(p,c,rng)`:
Use `top_K`. Need avoid sorting huge every iter? c size 2n-1 <=6000, sorting okay. But many iters; sorting 6000 ~ small. Use argsort if needed. For speed, use threshold and if near count > pool, use `np.argpartition(-c, pool_size)[:pool_size]` then sort small? Top K. Implement:
```
def compute_subgradient(p, c, rng):
    n=p.size; L=c.size
    cmax=float(c.max())
    tol = max(1e-15, cmax*1e-6)
    near_mask = c >= cmax - tol
    idx = np.flatnonzero(near_mask)
    top_K = int(max(8, min(160, n // 12)))  # for n=512 42, n=2048 160? min160
    pool_size = int(max(top_K*2, 32))
    if idx.size > pool_size:
        if rng.random() < 0.5:
            # top pool by c
            part = np.argpartition(-c, min(pool_size-1, L-1))[:pool_size]
            # sort part by c descending
            part = part[np.argsort(-c[part])]
            if idx.size <= pool_size*4:
                idx_use = idx[rng.choice(idx, size=pool_size, replace=False)]? need stable
            else:
                idx_use = part
        else:
            idx_use = idx[rng.choice(idx, size=pool_size, replace=False)]
        # choose top_K from idx_use: if top, first K; if random, random K
        if idx_use.size > top_K:
            if rng.random()<0.5:
                # highest c in idx_use
                idx_use = idx_use[np.argsort(-c[idx_use])[:top_K]]
            else:
                idx_use = idx_use[rng.choice(idx_use, size=top_K, replace=False)]
    idx = idx_use if 'idx_use' in locals()? Need careful.
```
Python scoping. Write straightforward:
```
    chosen = idx
    if chosen.size > pool_size:
       if rng.random() < 0.5:
           part = np.argpartition(-c, min(pool_size, L-1))[:pool_size]
           part = part[np.argsort(-c[part])]
           chosen = part
       else:
           chosen = chosen[rng.choice(chosen, size=pool_size, replace=False)]
    if chosen.size > top_K:
       if rng.random() < 0.5:
           order = np.argsort(-c[chosen])
           chosen = chosen[order[:top_K]]
       else:
           chosen = chosen[rng.choice(chosen, size=top_K, replace=False)]

`exp_step(p, G, lr)`:
```
def exp_step(p, G, lr):
    step = np.exp(-lr * np.clip(G, -50, 50))
    q = p * step
    s = q.sum()
    if not np.isfinite(s) or s <= 0:
       return p
    q /= s
    # floor if min 0
    mn = q.min()
    if mn <= 1e-300:
       q = q + 1e-15
       q /= q.sum()
    return q
```
Could use floor fraction each time: `eps=1e-12; q=(1-eps)*q+eps/n` maybe ensures all positive. Let's do:
```
eps = 1e-12
q = (1.0-eps)*q + eps/n
q /= q.sum()
```
This perturbs extremely small; fine.

`random_perturb(p, rng, strength)`:
```
def random_perturb(p, rng, strength):
    n=p.size
    logp=np.log(np.maximum(p,1e-300))
    # global noise
    logp += strength*0.2*rng.normal(0,1,n)
    # blocks
    nblocks = int(rng.integers(1, max(2, n//32 +1)))
    for _ in range(nblocks):
       maxw=max(2, n//8)
       minw=max(2, n//512)
       if minw>maxw: minw=maxw
       w=int(rng.integers(minw, maxw+1))
       st=int(rng.integers(0, max(1, n-w)))
       logp[st:st+w] += strength * rng.normal(0, 1.0)
    # maybe replace a random fraction with a new base? not here
    new=softmax_vec(logp, 1e-10)
    return new
```
Need if n small, rng.integers min>max handle.

`mirror_local_search(p0, time_limit, rng, max_no_imp=200)`:
Use top-level helpers. Time check:
```
def mirror_local_search(p0, time_limit, rng, max_no_imp=200):
    p = clean_sequence(p0)
    n = p.size
    if n<8: p = clean_sequence(base_power(n,0.5,0.5))
    best_p = p.copy()
    best_score = score_normalized(p)
    if not np.isfinite(best_score):
       return best_p, best_score
    end = time.monotonic() + max(0.2, float(time_limit))
    start = time.monotonic()
    no_imp = 0
    last_iter_time = start
    # step lrs maybe adapt
    lrs = (0.02, 0.05, 0.1, 0.2, 0.5, 1.0, 2.0)
    while time.monotonic() < end:
        c = convolution_fast(p)
        sc = float(2.0 * n * np.max(c))
        if sc < best_score - 1e-13:
            best_score = sc
            best_p = p.copy()
            no_imp = 0
        else:
            no_imp += 1
        if no_imp >= max_no_imp:
            break
        G = compute_subgradient(p, c, rng)
        improved = False
        # if G all zero, perturb
        for lr in lrs:
            q = exp_step(p, G, lr)
            qs = score_normalized(q)
            if np.isfinite(qs) and qs < best_score - 1e-13:
                best_score = qs
                best_p = q.copy()
                p = q
                no_imp = 0
                improved = True
                break
        if not improved:
            # random perturb attempts in increasing strength
            for st in (0.03, 0.08, 0.15, 0.3, 0.6):
                q = random_perturb(p, rng, st)
                qs = score_normalized(q)
                if np.isfinite(qs) and qs < best_score - 1e-13:
                    best_score = qs
                    best_p = q.copy()
                    p = q
                    no_imp = 0
                    improved = True
                    break
        if not improved:
            if no_imp > 30:
                # restart from best with noise
                if rng.random() < 0.25:
                    st = 0.05 + 0.35 * (no_imp / max_no_imp)
                    p = random_perturb(best_p, rng, st)
                    no_imp = 0
            # To avoid infinite tiny time, small sleep? no.
    # final polish from best with smaller steps? maybe
    if time.monotonic() < end - 0.5:
       p = best_p.copy()
       # do a quick deterministic local pass? optional
    return best_p.copy(), best_score
```

`nelder_mead_search(p0, time_limit, rng=None)`:
```
def nelder_mead_search(p0, time_limit, rng=None):
    p = clean_sequence(p0)
    n=p.size
    if n > 256: return p, score_normalized(p)
    try: from scipy.optimize import minimize
    except Exception: return p, score_normalized(p)
    y = np.log(np.maximum(p,1e-15)) - np.log(p).mean()
    # maxfev based
    if n <= 32: maxfev=3000
    elif n <= 64: maxfev=2000
    elif n <= 128: maxfev=1000
    else: maxfev=500
    maxfev = max(100, int(maxfev * min(1.0, time_limit/20.0)))
    res = minimize(nm_objective, y, method='Nelder-Mead', options={'maxiter': maxfev*10, 'maxfev': maxfev, 'xatol':1e-8, 'fatol':1e-8, 'adaptive':True})
    if res is None: return ...
    p2=softmax_vec(res.x, 1e-10)
    s2=score_normalized(p2)
    return p2,s2
```
`nm_objective` top-level:
```
def nm_objective(y):
    y=np.asarray(y,float)
    if y.size<1 or not np.all(np.isfinite(y)): return 1e6
    p=softmax_vec(y, 1e-10)
    sc=score_normalized(p)
    if not np.isfinite(sc): return 1.0e6
    return float(sc)
```
This exact max. Good.

`base_kernel_power` might create many good initial. Let's implement robust:
```
def base_kernel_mix(n, rng):
    base = base_power(n, 0.5, 0.5, increasing=False)
    m = min(n, max(8, int(n // 20)))
    # choose kernel as mixture of two powers
    m1 = int(max(2, m//3)); m2=m
    q1 = base_power(m, rng.uniform(0.2,1.5), 0.5, increasing=False)
    q2 = base_power(m, rng.uniform(0.2,1.5), 0.5, increasing=True)
    mix = rng.uniform(0,1)
    q = (1-mix)*q1 + mix*q2
    # apply kernel to base via convolution
    a = np.convolve(base, q)[:n]
    return clean_sequence(a)
```
If m small, convolution stable. This is low-cost.

Maybe define `density_from_weights(w)` where weights length defines basis: `R = len(w)`, `n = 1024` fixed? Hardcode n=1024. Not flexible. Could pass n as part of weights? e.g., y = [logn?]; no.
</think>

```python
"""
Search for a nonnegative step-function height sequence minimizing

    2 * n * max(conv(a,a)) / sum(a)^2

The optimizer uses:
- many asymmetric low-dimensional starting families (power-law, ramp, geometric,
  kernel-blurred power-law, random smooth shapes),
- a multiplicative / natural-gradient local search that repeatedly lowers a
  randomly chosen subset of near-maximal convolution coefficients,
- short Nelder-Mead polish on small lengths,
- periodic up/down-sampling and random restarts.
All returned sequences are nonnegative and normalized to sum 1.
"""

import numpy as np
import time


def clean_sequence(a):
    """Convert to a finite nonnegative 1D array normalized to sum 1."""
    arr = np.asarray(a, dtype=np.float64).ravel()
    if arr.size == 0:
        arr = np.ones(64, dtype=np.float64)
    arr[~np.isfinite(arr)] = 0.0
    arr = np.maximum(arr, 0.0)
    s = float(arr.sum())
    if s <= 1e-15:
        arr = np.ones(arr.size, dtype=np.float64) / float(arr.size)
    else:
        arr = arr / s
    return arr


def convolution_fast(a):
    """Full linear convolution a*a, with max numerical negatives clipped."""
    a = np.asarray(a, dtype=np.float64).ravel()
    n = a.size
    if n == 0:
        return np.array([], dtype=np.float64)
    need = 2 * n - 1
    if n <= 512:
        c = np.convolve(a, a)
    else:
        m = 1 << (need - 1).bit_length()
        fa = np.fft.rfft(a, m)
        c = np.fft.irfft(fa * fa, m)[:need]
    c = np.maximum(c, 0.0)
    return c


def score_normalized(p):
    """Exact score for normalized or auto-normalized nonnegative p."""
    p = np.asarray(p, dtype=np.float64).ravel()
    n = p.size
    if n == 0:
        return float("inf")
    if not np.all(np.isfinite(p)):
        return float("inf")
    s = float(p.sum())
    if s <= 1e-15:
        return float("inf")
    q = p / s
    c = convolution_fast(q)
    if c.size == 0:
        return float("inf")
    return float(2.0 * n * np.max(c))


def softmax_vec(v, floor_frac=0.0):
    """Numerically stable softmax with optional absolute floor."""
    v = np.asarray(v, dtype=np.float64).ravel()
    n = v.size
    if n == 0:
        return np.array([], dtype=np.float64)
    if not np.all(np.isfinite(v)):
        return np.ones(n, dtype=np.float64) / n
    v = v - np.max(v)
    z = np.exp(v)
    s = float(z.sum())
    if not np.isfinite(s) or s <= 1e-300:
        p = np.ones(n, dtype=np.float64) / n
    else:
        p = z / s
    if floor_frac > 0.0:
        p = (1.0 - floor_frac) * p + floor_frac / float(n)
    return p


def base_power(n, alpha, shift=0.5, increasing=False):
    """One-sided power-law density."""
    i = np.arange(n, dtype=np.float64) + max(float(shift), 1e-6)
    if increasing:
        v = np.exp(max(float(alpha), 0.0) * np.log(i))
    else:
        v = np.exp(-max(float(alpha), 1e-6) * np.log(i))
    v = np.maximum(v, 0.0)
    s = float(v.sum())
    if s <= 1e-300:
        return np.ones(n, dtype=np.float64) / n
    return v / s


def base_linear(n, increasing=True):
    """Linear ramp."""
    i = np.arange(n, dtype=np.float64) + 0.5
    if not increasing:
        i = i[::-1]
    return clean_sequence(i)


def base_geometric(n, r=0.97):
    """Geometric decay, decreasing endpoint."""
    i = np.arange(n, dtype=np.float64)
    r = min(max(float(r), 0.01), 0.999999)
    v = np.exp(np.log(r) * i)
    v = np.maximum(v, 1e-300)
    return clean_sequence(v)


def base_random(n, rng, strength=1.0):
    """Smooth random affine trend plus noise, exponentiated."""
    a = rng.uniform(-2.0, 2.0)
    b = rng.uniform(-1.0, 1.0)
    t = np.linspace(0.0, 1.0, n)
    y = a * t + b * strength * 0.1 + strength * rng.normal(0.0, 1.0, n)
    return softmax_vec(y, 1e-10)


def base_kernel_mix(n, rng):
    """Convolve a basic power-law with a random positive kernel.

    This produces a large family of nonnegative sequences with richer shapes
    than a bare power law while remaining cheap to generate.
    """
    base = base_power(n, 0.5, 0.5, increasing=False)
    m = int(min(n, max(8, n // 16)))
    q1 = base_power(m, rng.uniform(0.2, 1.6), 0.5, increasing=False)
    q2 = base_power(m, rng.uniform(0.2, 1.6), 0.5, increasing=True)
    mix = float(rng.uniform(0.0, 1.0))
    q = (1.0 - mix) * q1 + mix * q2
    noise = rng.normal(0.0, 0.15, m)
    q = q + 0.05 * softmax_vec(noise, 0.5)
    q = np.maximum(q, 0.0)
    qs = float(q.sum())
    if qs <= 1e-300:
        q = np.ones(m, dtype=np.float64) / m
    else:
        q = q / qs
    out = np.convolve(base, q)[:n]
    return clean_sequence(out)


def compute_subgradient(p, c, rng):
    """
    Natural-gradient direction (scaled by n) for reducing a chosen set of
    near-maximal convolution coefficients.

    Returns G such that p_i * exp(-lr*G_i) moves mass away from heavy
    contributions to the selected lags.
    """
    n = p.size
    L = c.size
    if L == 0 or n == 0:
        return r = None  # unreachable
    cmax = float(np.max(c))
    tol = max(1e-15, 1e-6 * cmax)
    near = c >= (cmax - tol)
    idx = np.flatnonzero(near)
    if idx.size == 0:
        idx = np.array([int(np.argmax(c))], dtype=int)

    top_k = int(max(8, min(180, n // 10)))
    pool_size = int(max(top_k * 2, 64))
    chosen = idx

    if chosen.size > pool_size:
        if rng.random() < 0.55:
            kth = min(pool_size, L) - 1
            part = np.argpartition(-c, kth)[:kth + 1]
            part = part[np.argsort(-c[part])]
            chosen = part
        else:
            chosen = chosen[rng.choice(chosen, size=pool_size, replace=False)]

    if chosen.size > top_k:
        if rng.random() < 0.5:
            order = np.argsort(-c[chosen])
            chosen = chosen[order[:top_k]]
        else:
            chosen = chosen[rng.choice(chosen, size=top_k, replace=False)]

    chosen = np.asarray(chosen, dtype=int).ravel()
    if chosen.size == 0:
        chosen = np.array([int(np.argmax(c))], dtype=int)

    s = np.zeros(n, dtype=np.float64)
    cnt = float(chosen.size)
    for k in chosen:
        k = int(k)
        lo = max(0, k - n + 1)
        hi = min(n - 1, k)
        if hi >= lo:
            j_min = k - hi
            j_max = k - lo
            s[lo:hi + 1] += p[j_min:j_max + 1][::-1]
    s /= cnt
    mean_c = float(np.mean(c[chosen]))
    g = float(n) * (s - mean_c)
    g = np.clip(g, -50.0, 50.0)

    if not np.all(np.isfinite(g)):
        g = np.zeros(n, dtype=np.float64)
    if float(np.max(g) - np.min(g)) < 1e-12:
        g = rng.normal(0.0, 0.1, n)
    return g


def exp_step(p, g, lr):
    """Multiplicative update on the simplex."""
    step = np.exp(-float(lr) * np.clip(g, -50.0, 50.0))
    q = p * step
    s = float(q.sum())
    if not np.isfinite(s) or s <= 1e-300:
        return p
    q = q / s
    q = (1.0 - 1e-12) * q + 1e-12 / float(q.size)
    return q / q.sum()


def random_perturb(p, rng, strength=0.1):
    """Random log-space block and global perturbation."""
    n = p.size
    logp = np.log(np.maximum(p, 1e-300))
    logp += 0.25 * float(strength) * rng.normal(0.0, 1.0, n)

    min_blocks = 1
    max_blocks = max(1, int(n // 32) + 1)
    nblocks = int(rng.integers(min_blocks, max_blocks + 1))
    min_w = max(2, int(n // 512))
    max_w = max(min_w, int(n // 8))
    for _ in range(int(nblocks)):
        if max_w <= min_w:
            w = min_w
        else:
            w = int(rng.integers(min_w, max_w + 1))
        if n <= 1:
            st = 0
        else:
            st = int(rng.integers(0, max(1, n - w + 1)))
        logp[st:st + w] += float(strength) * rng.normal(0.0, 1.0)

    return softmax_vec(logp, 1e-10)


def mirror_local_search(p0, time_limit, rng, max_no_imp=220):
    """
    Multiplicative local search with line search and random restarts.
    """
    p = clean_sequence(p0)
    n = p.size
    if n < 8:
        p = base_power(n, 0.5, 0.5, increasing=False)
        n = p.size

    best_p = p.copy()
    best_score = score_normalized(p)
    if not np.isfinite(best_score):
        return best_p, best_score

    end = time.monotonic() + max(0.2, float(time_limit))
    lrs = (0.01, 0.02, 0.05, 0.10, 0.20, 0.45, 1.0, 2.0)
    no_imp = 0

    while time.monotonic() < end:
        c = convolution_fast(p)
        sc = float(2.0 * n * np.max(c))
        if sc < best_score - 1e-13:
            best_score = sc
            best_p = p.copy()
            no_imp = 0
        else:
            no_imp += 1

        if no_imp >= max_no_imp:
            break

        g = compute_subgradient(p, c, rng)
        improved = False

        # Deterministic / natural-gradient line search.
        for lr in lrs:
            q = exp_step(p, g, lr)
            qs = score_normalized(q)
            if np.isfinite(qs) and qs < best_score - 1e-13:
                best_score = qs
                best_p = q.copy()
                p = q
                no_imp = 0
                improved = True
                break

        # If stuck, try random log-space perturbations.
        if not improved:
            for st in (0.03, 0.08, 0.15, 0.30, 0.60):
                q = random_perturb(p, rng, st)
                qs = score_normalized(q)
                if np.isfinite(qs) and qs < best_score - 1e-13:
                    best_score = qs
                    best_p = q.copy()
                    p = q
                    no_imp = 0
                    improved = True
                    break

        # Occasionally restart from the incumbent with noise.
        if not improved and no_imp > 30 and rng.random() < 0.30:
            st = 0.05 + 0.40 * (float(no_imp) / float(max_no_imp))
            p = random_perturb(best_p, rng, st)
            no_imp = 0

    return best_p.copy(), best_score


def upsample_sequence(p, factor=2, max_len=4096):
    """Linear interpolation upsampling."""
    p = np.asarray(p, dtype=np.float64).ravel()
    n = p.size
    if n == 0:
        return p
    m = int(min(max_len, max(n + 1, n * int(factor))))
    x0 = np.linspace(0.0, 1.0, n)
    x1 = np.linspace(0.0, 1.0, m)
    q = np.interp(x1, x0, p)
    return clean_sequence(q)


def downsample_sequence(p, factor=2, min_len=24):
    """Average blocks by integer factor."""
    p = np.asarray(p, dtype=np.float64).ravel()
    n = p.size
    if n <= int(min_len):
        return p
    m = max(int(min_len), int(n // int(factor)))
    out = np.zeros(m, dtype=np.float64)
    for i in range(m):
        a = int(i * n // m)
        b = int((i + 1) * n // m)
        if b > a:
            out[i] = float(np.mean(p[a:b]))
    return clean_sequence(out)


def nm_objective(y):
    """Objective for Nelder-Mead on log-weights."""
    y = np.asarray(y, dtype=np.float64).ravel()
    if y.size == 0 or not np.all(np.isfinite(y)):
        return 1.0e6
    p = softmax_vec(y, 1e-10)
    sc = score_normalized(p)
    if not np.isfinite(sc):
        return 1.0e6
    return float(sc)


def nelder_mead_search(p0, time_limit):
    """Small-length exact-polish search. Returns (p, score)."""
    p = clean_sequence(p0)
    n = p.size
    base_score = score_normalized(p)

    if n > 192:
        return p, base_score

    try:
        from scipy.optimize import minimize
    except Exception:
        return p, base_score

    y = np.log(np.maximum(p, 1e-150))
    y = y - float(np.mean(y))

    if n <= 32:
        maxfev = 3500
    elif n <= 64:
        maxfev = 2500
    elif n <= 128:
        maxfev = 1200
    else:
        maxfev = 650

    maxfev = max(150, int(maxfev * min(1.0, max(0.2, float(time_limit) / 25.0))))

    try:
        res = minimize(
            nm_objective,
            y,
            method="Nelder-Mead",
            options={
                "maxiter": int(maxfev * 8),
                "maxfev": int(maxfev),
                "xatol": 1e-8,
                "fatol": 1e-9,
                "adaptive": True,
            },
        )
        if res is not None and hasattr(res, "x") and np.all(np.isfinite(np.asarray(res.x, dtype=np.float64))):
            p2 = softmax_vec(np.asarray(res.x, dtype=np.float64), 1e-10)
            s2 = score_normalized(p2)
            return p2, s2
    except Exception:
        pass

    return p, base_score


def _initial_bases(n, rng):
    """Generate a diverse set of nonnegative starting sequences."""
    out = []
    for alpha in (0.30, 0.50, 0.70, 0.95):
        out.append(base_power(n, alpha, 0.5, increasing=False))
    for alpha in (0.80, 1.10, 1.50, 2.20):
        out.append(base_power(n, alpha, 0.5, increasing=True))
    out.append(base_linear(n, increasing=True))
    out.append(base_linear(n, increasing=False))
    out.append(base_geometric(n, 0.97))
    out.append(base_geometric(n, 0.88))
    out.append(base_random(n, rng, 0.5))
    out.append(base_random(n, rng, 1.0))
    out.append(base_random(n, rng, 2.0))
    out.append(base_kernel_mix(n, rng))
    out.append(base_kernel_mix(n, rng))
    out.append(base_kernel_mix(n, rng))
    return out


def propose_candidate(seed=42, budget_s=1800, **kwargs):
    """
    Run the search within `budget_s` and return the best nonnegative sequence
    found as a list of floats.
    """
    seed = int(seed)
    budget_s = max(5.0, float(budget_s))
    deadline = time.monotonic() + (budget_s - 5.0)
    rng = np.random.default_rng(seed)

    best_seq = None
    best_score = float("inf")

    # Optionally start from the supplied previous construction.
    parent = globals().get("height_sequence_1", None)
    if parent is not None:
        pp = clean_sequence(parent)
        sp = score_normalized(pp)
        if np.isfinite(sp) and sp < best_score:
            best_seq = pp
            best_score = sp
        print(f"[init] parent score={sp:.9f} len={pp.size}")

    # Small to medium lengths: enough additive attack surface, still fast.
    n_values = [16, 24, 32, 48, 64, 96, 128, 192, 256, 384, 512, 768, 1024, 1536, 2048]
    initial_seq = {}
    initial_score = {}

    for n in n_values:
        if time.monotonic() > deadline:
            break
        init_p = None
        init_s = float("inf")
        for b in _initial_bases(n, rng):
            b = clean_sequence(b)
            sc = score_normalized(b)
            if np.isfinite(sc) and sc < init_s:
                init_s = sc
                init_p = b
        if init_p is not None:
            initial_seq[n] = init_p
            initial_score[n] = init_s
            if init_s < best_score:
                best_score = init_s
                best_seq = init_p.copy()

    if best_seq is None:
        best_seq = clean_sequence(base_power(128, 0.5, 0.5, increasing=False))
        best_score = score_normalized(best_seq)

    print(f"[init] best score={best_score:.9f} len={best_seq.size}")

    # Stage 1: quick Nelder-Mead polish for small lengths.
    small_ns = [16, 24, 32, 48, 64, 96, 128]
    if best_score > 1.52 and time.monotonic() < deadline:
        for n in small_ns:
            if time.monotonic() > deadline - 20.0:
                break
            if n not in initial_seq:
                continue
            rem = deadline - time.monotonic()
            tlim = min(18.0, rem / 6.0)
            if tlim <= 2.0:
                break
            p2, s2 = nelder_mead_search(initial_seq[n], tlim)
            if np.isfinite(s2) and s2 < best_score:
                best_score = s2
                best_seq = p2.copy()
                initial_seq[n] = p2.copy()
                initial_score[n] = s2
                print(f"[nm] n={n} score={s2:.9f}")

    # Stage 2: main multiplicative local-search loop.
    # Sort lengths by initial score and cycle through them, while biasing
    # toward the current global best.
    items = []
    for idx, n in enumerate(n_values):
        if n in initial_seq:
            items.append((float(initial_score[n]), n, idx))
    items.sort()

    order_ns = [n for _, n, _ in items]
    if best_seq is not None and best_seq.size not in order_ns:
        order_ns.append(int(best_seq.size))

    cycle_idx = 0
    iterations = 0
    target = 1.5027

    while time.monotonic() < deadline - 8.0:
        rem = deadline - time.monotonic()
        if rem < 10.0:
            break

        # Choose length: exploit best, otherwise explore sorted order.
        if iterations % 5 == 0 and best_seq is not None:
            n = int(best_seq.size)
        else:
            n = int(order_ns[cycle_idx % len(order_ns)])
            cycle_idx += 1

        if n <= 0:
            continue

        # Allocate time per length. Larger lengths get longer runs but fewer.
        if n <= 64:
            tlim = 22.0
        elif n <= 256:
            tlim = 32.0
        elif n <= 1024:
            tlim = 45.0
        else:
            tlim = 60.0
        tlim = min(tlim, rem - 4.0)
        if tlim <= 3.0:
            break

        # Starting point.
        if best_seq is not None and n == int(best_seq.size):
            base = best_seq
        elif n in initial_seq:
            base = initial_seq[n]
        else:
            base = base_power(n, 0.5, 0.5, increasing=False)

        # Freshen the start: usually keep incumbent for best length, otherwise
        # occasionally perturb to escape its basin.
        if best_seq is not None and n == int(best_seq.size):
            if iterations % 7 == 0:
                start = random_perturb(base, rng, 0.03 + 0.08 * float(rng.random()))
            else:
                start = base.copy()
        else:
            if rng.random() < 0.55:
                start = random_perturb(base, rng, 0.06 + 0.20 * float(rng.random()))
            else:
                start = base.copy()

        p2, s2 = mirror_local_search(start, tlim, rng)

        if np.isfinite(s2) and s2 < best_score:
            best_score = s2
            best_seq = p2.copy()
            print(f"[local] improved n={p2.size} score={s2:.9f} t={time.monotonic():.1f}")

        if n in initial_seq and (n not in initial_score or s2 < initial_score[n]):
            initial_seq[n] = p2.copy()
            initial_score[n] = s2

        # Occasional up/down sampling from best to search nearby resolution.
        if iterations % 9 == 0 and best_seq is not None and rem > 90.0:
            op = upsample_sequence(best_seq, factor=2, max_len=4096)
            if op.size != best_seq.size:
                tlim2 = min(30.0, rem - 8.0)
                pu, su = mirror_local_search(op, tlim2, rng)
                if np.isfinite(su) and su < best_score:
                    best_score = su
                    best_seq = pu.copy()
                    print(f"[upsample] improved n={pu.size} score={su:.9f}")
            dp = downsample_sequence(best_seq, factor=2, min_len=32)
            if dp.size != best_seq.size:
                tlim2 = min(18.0, rem - 4.0)
                pd, sd = mirror_local_search(dp, tlim2, rng)
                if np.isfinite(sd) and sd < best_score:
                    best_score = sd
                    best_seq = pd.copy()
                    print(f"[downsample] improved n={pd.size} score={sd:.9f}")

        iterations += 1

    # Final polish if time remains.
    rem = deadline - time.monotonic()
    if rem > 8.0 and best_seq is not None:
        p2, s2 = mirror_local_search(best_seq, rem - 2.0, rng, max_no_imp=120)
        if np.isfinite(s2) and s2 < best_score:
            best_score = s2
            best_seq = p2.copy()

    # Ensure valid final representation.
    if best_seq is None:
        best_seq = clean_sequence(base_power(128, 0.5, 0.5, increasing=False))
    else:
        best_seq = clean_sequence(best_seq)

    # If the exact evaluator is available and sequence is not too large,
    # perform one authoritative check. This cannot hurt and avoids rare
    # FFT rounding artifacts in the returned incumbent.
    try:
        if len(best_seq) <= 4096:
            exact = evaluate_sequence([float(x) for x in best_seq])
            if np.isfinite(exact):
                print(f"[final] local_bound={best_score:.9f} exact={exact:.9f} len={len(best_seq)}")
            else:
                print("[final] evaluate invalid, replacing with fallback")
                best_seq = clean_sequence(base_power(128, 0.5, 0.5, increasing=False))
        else:
            print(f"[final] long sequence len={len(best_seq)}, skipping exact evaluator")
    except Exception:
        pass

    return [float(max(0.0, x)) for x in best_seq]
```
